  :276 ## §7 — TERM 3'S BUILD STATE *(added 2026-08-26, b193; ### **THE PLAN RETIRED 2026-08-26, b194**)*
  :277 
  :278 > # ⛔ ### **THE PLAN IS RETIRED AT ITS OWN FALSIFIER — 2026-08-26 (b194).**
  :279 >
  :280 > ### **The `E₁`-units cannot supply what a restricted tensor product needs, and the record said so eleven acts before the plan was written.**
  :281 >
  :282 > **`relay/reports/2026-08-19-e1-unit-purity.md`, at content:**
  :283 > · ### **“E₁(3,1) AND E₁(2,2) CONTAIN NO NONZERO SCHMIDT-PURE VECTOR AT ALL”** — and of (3,1): *“the unit is UNIQUE up to scale… ### **no choice exists to make**”*;
  :284 > · cell **(2,1)**: `d₁ = 0` — ### **NO-UNIT**, *“the projector image is 0 exactly”*;
  :285 > · the units are a ### **CHOICE CLASS** — *“the units exist; **no canonical choice is claimed**”*;
  :286 > · overall: ### **“(b) LANDS — MIXED, at every cell that has units at all.”**
  :287 >
  :288 > **A restricted tensor product `⊗′_v (H_v, e_v)` requires (i) a vector at every place, (ii) that vector **pure**, (iii) of norm one, (iv) **chosen**.** ### **(i), (ii) and (iv) each fail on the record's own reading. Any one retires the plan; all three hold.**
  :289 >
  :290 > *** ### **AND THE FAILURE IS STRONGER THAN THE FALSIFIER b193 WROTE.** b193 said *“not isometries”*, which presumes the maps can be formed and asks whether they preserve norms. ### **The maps cannot be formed at all — there is no pure distinguished vector to tensor with. That is not a weaker isometry; it is an absent map.** *The clause that fires is the falsifier's second: “the record's E₁-units do not supply what a directed system of Hilbert spaces requires.”* ***
  :291 >
  :292 > ### **NOT PATCHED. NOT TUNED. NO ALTERNATIVE CONSTRUCTION IS PROPOSED** — the stop-loss forbids it and ### **the re-scope is the author's.**
  :293 >
  :294 > ### **LAYER ONE IS KEPT AND IS NOT WITHDRAWN.** *It compiled; what it states is true: given two factors carrying the shape, the tensor carries it.* ### **What is retired is the route that would have used it, not the theorem.**

  :374 
  :375 ### **THE CLASSICAL READ (P2) — LOCATED, AT ITS SOURCE DOCUMENT**
  :376 
  :377 **J. von Neumann, *On infinite direct products*, Compositio Mathematica, tome 6 (1939), pp. 1–77** — numdam PDF, 78 pages, extracted locally, ### **served 2026-08-26. EXTERNAL.**
  :378 
  :379 > **Chapter 4's title:** *“Decomposition of the complete direct product into incomplete ones.”*
  :380 > **DEFINITION 4.1.1, quoted:** *“If ℬ is an equivalence-class, then let ∏⊗_{α∈I}ℬ_α be the closed linear set determined by all ∏⊗_{α∈I} f_α where (f_α, α∈I) is **any C₀-sequence from ℬ**… This is an **incomplete direct product**… the **ℬ-adic** incomplete direct product.”*
  :381 > **LEMMA 4.1.1, quoted:** the incomplete products for all ℬ are *“**mutually orthogonal**, and the closed linear set they determine is”* the complete one.
  :382 
  :383 ### **So: the COMPLETE product needs no distinguished vector. An INCOMPLETE one needs an equivalence class of C₀-sequences — and NOWHERE does it require those vectors to be PURE or ELEMENTARY in any bipartition.** ### **PURITY IS NOT VON NEUMANN'S REQUIREMENT.**

  :463 
  :464 > ### **“A sequence `f_α`, `α ∈ I`, is a **C₀-sequence**, if and only if `f_α ∈ ℬ_α` for all
  :465 > `α ∈ I`, and **`Σ_{α∈I} | ‖f_α‖ − 1 |` converges**.”**

  :468 mutually disjoint equivalence-classes” *— and Definition 4.1.1, which builds the incomplete

  :553 (`S̄ = E₁ ⊕ E₋₁ ⊕ Eᵢ ⊕ E₋ᵢ`) and a **positivity** (`B(f,f) = ‖f‖²` on `E₁`).
  :554 ### **Positive-definiteness on `E₁` is VACUOUS if `E₁ = 0`.** ### **The nonvanishing is not in
  :555 the theorem.** It is assembled from Q2's **parenthetical** — *“(`ι` is `F`-equivariant, banked
  :556 exact, **so each `E_λ(S̄)` is the closure of its level tower**)”* — together with a level
  :557 dimension from elsewhere. ***This section credits Q2 with what it proves and no more.***

  :1046 ## §16 — THE TRANSFORM CONVENTION, AND THE SIGN THAT IS LEFT *(added 2026-08-26, b203)*
  :1047 
  :1048 > ### **READS AND ONE VERDICT. No value derived or approached. THE `Fφ = ξ` FENCE STOOD.**

  :1050 
  :1051 ### **(P1) THE CONVENTION QUESTION**
  :1052 
  :1053 **(i) Does `F_eR` square to the identity on the relevant space? ### UNSTATED BY THE SOURCE.**
  :1054 Searched at content: *involut* **0**, *self-dual* **0**, *Plancherel* **0**, *parity* **2 — both
  :1055 about `W`**; and the 2025 paper has *parity* **0**. ### **Derivable by Fourier inversion — an
  :1056 IMPORT — and held by the corpus at b19's model-exact check and b32's longhand.**
  :1057 ### **§15's construction is marked conditional above, with its condition named.**
  :1058 
  :1059 **(ii) Is `F_eR` the corpus's `F`? ### MATCHED-UP-TO-A-NAMED-NORMALIZATION**, in two parts:
  :1060 ### **the exponent's SIGN** — the corpus's `exp(+2πi·m_j m_k/N)/√N` against the source's
  :1061 `exp(−2πixy)`; ### **they differ by parity, so they agree ON EVENS**, and Sonin's space is
  :1062 inside `L²(ℝ)_ev` — ***but that step is the same import as (i) and is graded as one, not waved
  :1063 through***; and ### **the DISCRETIZATION** — a finite centered DFT on a self-dual grid, declared
  :1064 by b19 to *“represent”* the continuum transform with *“convergence in `N` reported, not
  :1065 assumed”*.
  :1066 
  :1067 **(iii) Is that the normalization G1 named? ### NO** — decided by comparing both at content.
  :1068 G1's four clauses are *“`λ = 1`; `c = 2π` enters as `(2πλ)²`; `W` is positive and orders the
  :1069 modes oppositely to `λ²`; **the layer's mode `n` is classical index `2n`**”* — ### **all four
  :1070 about the operator's parameter, the bandwidth, the ordering and the INDEXING. Not one is a
  :1071 transform convention; not one is an eigenfunction scalar.**
  :1072 
  :1073 > *** ### **THE STRUCTURAL FINDING: THREE DISTINCT NORMALIZATIONS ARE IN PLAY AND THE CORPUS HAS
  :1074 > BEEN CALLING ALL THREE “NORMALIZATION”.** ### **(1) G1's** — operator, bandwidth, ordering,
  :1075 > mode index; owner the crown act. ### **(2) the transform convention** — exponent sign and
  :1076 > discretization; owners b19 and eq. (5); ***named for the first time this act***. ### **(3) the
  :1077 > eigenfunction scalar** — b202's.
  :1078 > ### **A claim matched up to one of them is not matched up to the others**, and a reader who saw
  :1079 > *“matched up to a named normalization”* in three places had no way to tell them apart. ***
  :1080 
  :1081 ### **(P2) THE OUTWARD READ**
  :1082 
  :1083 ### **`[13]` RESOLVED** from the source's own reference list — Richard-Jung, Ramis, Thomann,

  :1089 > *New eigenfunctions for the negative part of the Connes–Moscovici prolate spectrum*, C. R.

  :1091 >
  :1092 > ### **Proposition 5, quoted:** *“Then we can reformulate [23, Proposition 14]: **We have
  :1093 > `V∓ₙ = n!(±2iπ)⁻ⁿ Uₙ`**”*, with `U₀ = 1` and `V∓₀ = 1`.
  :1094 > ### **DOES NOT SETTLE the scalar — both leading coefficients are normalized to 1 BY FIAT**, so
  :1095 > the relation is between coefficient sequences at fixed normalizations, not a ratio between the
  :1096 > two `L²` functions. ***At `n = 0` it reads `1 = 1`, which is the fiat restated.***
  :1097

  :1101 > eigenvalue of `W_{Λ,sa}` and `φ` a corresponding eigenfunction. Then … **(ii) the dimension of
  :1102 > the eigenspace `E_μ` is ONE**”.* ***
  :1103 > ### **And the same paper normalizes `ξ` ONCE** — *“`ξ_{μ,Λ}(x) = −sin(2πΛx)/(πx) + O(1/x²)`”*
  :1104 > — ### **resolving, from CM's own cited authors, the source-internal ambiguity b202 found.**
  :1105 > ***b202's finding stands as a finding about CM's TEXT; it is not a defect in the mathematics.***
  :1106

  :1108 
  :1109 | step | grade |
  :1110 |:--|:--|
  :1111 | `W_sa` commutes with `F` | **HELD** — CM Thm 2.6(i); RRT Thm 3(i) |
  :1112 | `φ_μ` a `W_sa`-eigenvector, negative `μ`, in the Sonin space, **even** | **HELD** — CM Cor 3.2, Lemma 3.1 |
  :1113 | **the eigenspace `E_μ` is one-dimensional** | ### **HELD — IMPORT**, RRT Lemma 2(ii) |
  :1114 | therefore `Fφ_μ = c·φ_μ` | ### **DERIVATION GRADE** |
  :1115 | `F² = 1` on evens, so `c = ±1` | ### **IMPORT** — unstated by either source |
  :1116 
  :1117 > ### **CONCLUSION: `φ_μ` IS AN `F`-EIGENVECTOR, AND LIES IN `E₁` OR `E₋₁` OF THE ARCHIMEDEAN
  :1118 > SONIN SPACE. b201's (M-a) IS DISCHARGED; (M-b), THE SIGN, IS NOT.**
  :1119 >
  :1120 > ### **THE FENCE HELD, AND HERE IS HOW: this is NOT `Fφ = ξ` read as `Fφ = φ`.** It is
  :1121 > `Fφ = cφ` **derived** from simplicity plus commutation, after which `ξ_μ` is **identified** as
  :1122 > `cφ_μ` rather than substituted for it. ***The two functions are still two functions; what is
  :1123 > new is that a scalar relates them.***
  :1124 
  :1125 ### **M22, RESTATED: IS `c = +1` OR `c = −1`?** ### **b202's sum test resolves into it exactly:
  :1126 `φ_μ + ξ_μ = (1+c)φ_μ`, which is `2φ_μ ≠ 0` if `c = +1` and `0` if `c = −1`.**
  :1127 ### **Exactly one of the two sectors contains `φ_μ`, and which one is the whole question.**
  :1128 
  :1129 ### **IS THE OBSTACLE A RESULT OR A CONVENTION? A RESULT** — the sign is a fact about the object.
  :1130 ### **But one CONDITION on the derivation is a convention, and its owner is named: `F² = 1` on
  :1131 evens, owned by the classical Fourier inversion theorem — an import, not this corpus's to rule,
  :1132 and unstated by both source documents.**
  :1133 
  :1134 ### **NO ROUTE IS DECLARED AVAILABLE ON AN UNSETTLED SCALAR.** ### **And the applicability caveat
  :1135 is now TRIPLY live, with its three parts finally distinct.**

  :1139 ## §17 — THE SIGN ACROSS THE FAMILY: BOTH VALUES OCCUR, AT THE WRONG PARAMETER *(added 2026-08-26, b204)*
  :1140 
  :1141 > ### **READS, A DERIVATION AND A BUILD. No value derived or approached. THE `Fφ = ξ` FENCE
  :1142 > STOOD.** *Core 314 → 320, every terminal zero-axiom, printed from its own run.*
  :1143 
  :1144 ### **(P1) WHAT THE SOURCES SAY**
  :1145 
  :1146 **The parametrization, quoted, and it decides the act:** *“The variable change reduces `W_Λ` to
  :1147 `D_τ`, **where `τ = 2πΛ²`**.”* ### **So `τ = 4π ⇔ Λ = √2`, and `Λ = 1 ⇔ τ = 2π`.**
  :1148 
  :1149 **(i) The Fourier eigenvalue.** CM `2112.05500v1`: ### **no**. RRT 2025: ### **yes — and the paper
  :1150 makes the identification itself.** ### **Proposition 6(ii)(b):** `ξ_{μ,Λ}` is the restriction of
  :1151 **the Fourier transform of the unique even solution `φ_{μ,Λ}`**, which agrees with `f_{μ,Λ}`
  :1152 where *“`f_{μ,Λ}(Λ) = 1`”* — ### **so `φ(Λ) = 1` and `φ ≠ 0`, by the source's own
  :1153 normalization.** ### **Proposition 7:** *“Let `μ` be an **even** CM-eigenvalue … **if `μ` is a non
  :1154 classical eigenvalue, then `ψ(Λ) = ±1`**.”*

  :1347 ## §20 — THE VARIABLE PASSAGE: DERIVED, AND IT IS NOT THE EXPLANATION *(added 2026-08-26, b206)*
  :1348 
  :1349 > *** ### **VERDICT: (NOT PINNED). The passage derives cleanly and is a PURE POSITIVE RESCALING.

  :1355 DEFECT IN THE PAPER:** *“The variable change `x = Λe^x` and `e^x = x` reduces `W_Λ` to `D_τ`,
  :1356 where `τ = 2πΛ²`”* — ### **two different variables both render as “`x`”.** ***So I did not guess
  :1357 its intent: I derived the passage from the two operator definitions, which are not garbled.***
  :1358 
  :1359 ### **(P1) THE DERIVATION**
  :1360 
  :1361 **The substitution: `x = Λ·u`** — a pure rescaling of the independent variable, `f(x) := g(x/Λ)`.
  :1362 Machine-checked with `sympy`, the difference printed:
  :1363 
  :1364 ```
  :1365 (W_Λ f)(Λu) = 4π²Λ⁴u² g + u² g'' + 2u g' − g''
  :1366 (D_τ  g)(u)  = 4π²Λ⁴u² g + u² g'' + 2u g' − g''      [τ = 2πΛ²]
  :1367 ### DIFFERENCE : 0
  :1368 ```
  :1369 
  :1370 ### **So `(W_Λ f)(Λu) = (D_τ g)(u)` exactly — and `τ = 2πΛ²` is RECOVERED, not assumed.**
  :1371 The eigenvalue `μ` is unchanged by the passage.
  :1372 
  :1373 | object | induced action |
  :1374 |:--|:--|
  :1375 | `ψ` | `ψ^D(u) = ` ### **`πΛ`** ` · ψ^W(Λu)` — ***positive for every `Λ > 0`*** |
  :1376 | `y_I` | `y_I^D(u) = y_I^W(Λu)` ### **exactly — no factor, no sign** |
  :1377 | the eigenfunction scale | `α^D = πΛ·α^W`, with `α^W = ±1` (Prop 7), so ### **`\|α^D\| = πΛ`** |
  :1378 
  :1379 ### **THE DERIVATION'S CHECKS — TWO PARAMETERS, NEITHER OF THEM OURS:**
  :1380 ### **`Λ = √2`:** predicted `π√2 = 4.442882938158366`; ### **b205 measured it to all 16 digits**,
  :1381 and ### **§4.2.3 prints the same number.** ***Two numbers the derivation did not use.***

  :1384 eigenvalues, ### **`\|α\|/(π√3) = 1.0000003, 1.0000001, 1.0000010`** — ### **and the alternation

  :1462

  :1464 sign alternates across the six reached. ### **So both values occur, and THE SONIN SECTOR AT
  :1465 `τ = 2π` IS NONZERO.**
  :1466 
  :1467 ### **THE GRADE, STATED AND NOT ROUNDED UP: BENCH.** A measured statement about a **six-element
  :1468 sweep at one parameter** from a float instrument with declared axes. ### **It is not a theorem and
  :1469 it is not called one.** ### **What a grade above bench would require, named: a proof that the
  :1470 alternation holds for the FAMILY** — no source states it (RRT's *“alternatively”* is prose beside
  :1471 a table), and this act does not attempt it.
  :1472

  :1474 print `−4.44288293889868`”*, stable on six settings, and b206 showed the passage cannot explain
  :1475 it. ### **It is unexplained. It is NOT resolved here and NOT folded into this verdict.**
  :1476 
  :1477 > *** ### **WHY THE VERDICT SURVIVES IT — SHOWN, NOT ASSERTED, AND MECHANIZED:**
  :1478 > the unexplained freedom is a **GLOBAL** sign — every `α` would carry the same unknown factor.

  :1512 ## §22 — THE ALTERNATION DERIVED: THE SONIN SECTOR IS NONZERO AT DERIVATION GRADE ON NAMED IMPORTS *(added 2026-08-27, b211)*
  :1513 
  :1514 > *** ### **VERDICT: (DERIVES).** `α(μ_k)·β′(μ_k) = ∫₁^∞ ψ² dx`, with `c₀ = +1`, **derived at
  :1515 > content** — and the bench relation b210 measured is the thing derived, not the thing cited.
  :1516 > ### **THE SONIN SECTOR IS NONZERO AT DERIVATION GRADE ON NAMED IMPORTS**, where §21 had it at
  :1517 > bench grade over six measured eigenvalues. *Core 327 → 339, every terminal zero-axiom.* ***
  :1518 >
  :1519 > ### **THIS IS A FACT ABOUT THE SONIN SECTOR. IT IS NOT A CLAIM THAT THE SONIN SECTOR IS WHAT
  :1520 > TERM 3 NEEDS** — §13's naming question is b200's routed question and ***stands with the
  :1521 > author, untouched.***
  :1522 
  :1523 ### **THE IDENTITY, AND WHERE THE POSITIVITY COMES FROM**
  :1524 
  :1525 The operator is formally self-adjoint with weight one: `(x²−1)f″ + 2xf′ = [(x²−1)f′]′`, so
  :1526 `D_τ − μ = 0` is `[p f′]′ + (q − μ)f = 0` with `p = x²−1`, `q = τ²x²`. The two-parameter identity
  :1527 `[p·W(u,v)]′ = (ν−μ)uv` gives, in the limit `ν → μ`, ### **`[p·W(u,u̇)]′ = u²` — and that square
  :1528 is the entire source of the positivity this section rests on.**
  :1529 
  :1530 Applied to `y_I` the boundary term at `x = 1` vanishes **twice over** (`p(1) = 0`, and `ẏ_I(1) = 0`
  :1531 because `y_I(1) ≡ 1` carries no `μ`); applied to `ψ` the term at `∞` vanishes because the leading
  :1532 asymptotic coefficient is `1` and carries no `μ`. At an eigenvalue `ψ = α y_I`, the two pieces
  :1533 combine and **the arbitrary evaluation point cancels** — as it must, since `β = p·W(ψ,y_I)` is
  :1534 constant in `x`. ### **That cancellation is an internal check of the algebra and it passes.**
  :1535 
  :1536 > **A detail worth keeping:** at `x₀ = √2`, `p = 1` *exactly*, so `β = W(ψ,y_I)(√2) = F`, the
  :1537 > paper's determinant, with no factor. ### **RRT's connection point is not arbitrary — it is the
  :1538 > point where the weight is one.**
  :1539 
  :1540 ### **THE FOUR CONSEQUENCES, AT THEIR GRADES**
  :1541 
  :1542 | # | consequence | grade | on |
  :1543 |:--|:--|:--|:--|
  :1544 | C1 | **every zero of `β` is simple** | ### **DERIVES** | the identity alone |
  :1545 | C2 | **`sign(α_k)` alternates** at consecutive even eigenvalues | ### **DERIVES** | premise (ii): `β` entire of order ≤ ½ |
  :1546 | C3 | **both values of `c` occur** | ### **DERIVES** | b203's chain (I8 + I6 + I10) |
  :1547 | C4 | ### **the Sonin sector is nonzero** | ### **DERIVES** | I9 (CM Cor 3.2), I7, I6, C3 |
  :1548 
  :1549 *** ### **AND C1 IS THE PAPER'S OWN OPEN CONJECTURE.** RRT §4.2.2, quoted: *"We **conjecture**
  :1550 that the zeros of `F` are simple. (This is supported by some numerical experiments.)"* ### **For
  :1551 the even family and on the premises named, this act derives it.** ***
  :1552 
  :1553 > ### **AND IT IS NOT LEMMA 2(ii).** The ferry's brief called the simplicity import *"what the
  :1554 > derivation will re-prove"*, but the source carries **two different simplicities**: Lemma 2(ii)
  :1555 > is that the **eigenspace** is one-dimensional — a *proved lemma*, which this derivation does
  :1556 > **not** use and does **not** re-prove, and which is still an import in C3. ### **The one
  :1557 > derived is the determinant-zero simplicity, which the source leaves open.** *Reported rather

  :1563 `y_I(x₀,μ)` **entire of order ≤ ½**) · `I2` RRT Prop 6(i) (Borel-summable) · `I3` RRT Prop 6(ii)

  :1706 ## §24 — THE ORIENTATION BITS: THE LADDER FIXED, AND b205 DECIDED *(added 2026-08-27, b214)*
  :1707 
  :1708 > *** ### **VERDICT: (FIXED).** `c_k = −(−i)^k` — ### **`ε = −1`, orientation `i^{−k}`** — measured
  :1709 > from `F φ / φ` **directly**, not inferred from `α`. ### **GRADE BENCH.**
  :1710 > ### **AND b205's `μ₋₂` DISCREPANCY IS DECIDED: `F φ / φ = +1` there, so the instrument's
  :1711 > `α = +π√2` is the sign satisfying `F φ = c φ` — and the decision is CONVENTION-FREE.** ***
  :1712 
  :1713 ### **THE CONVENTION, PINNED AND STATED ONCE**
  :1714 
  :1715 `(F f)(y) = ∫ f(x) e^{+2πixy} dx` — the continuum limit of b19's centered DFT
  :1716 (`F[j,k] = exp(2πi·m_j·m_k/N)/√N`, self-dual spacing). ### **The keystone does NOT pick the sign:**
  :1717 `F² = parity, F⁴ = 1` holds for **both** exponents, so it constrains `F` without choosing the
  :1718 convention. *The sign is adopted here, not derived — and the record contains both (b19 positive,
  :1719 b71's `χ_∞` conjugate).*
  :1720 
  :1721 ### **THE TWO BITS**
  :1722 
  :1723 | rank | family | `c` measured from `F φ / φ` |
  :1724 |:--|:--|:--|
  :1725 | 1 | ODD | ### **`+i`** |
  :1726 | 2 | EVEN | ### **`+1`** |
  :1727 
  :1728 With each family's alternation from b211 and b212, those two values **fix every rank**, and the
  :1729 whole pattern reconciles with b212's measured `− + + −`.
  :1730 
  :1731 *** ### **AND `ε` IS CONVENTION-FREE WHILE THE ORIENTATION IS NOT.** Under b19's convention
  :1732 `c_k = −i^{−k}`; under the conjugate, `c_k = −i^{+k}`. ### **`ε = −1` either way.** *That is
  :1733 exactly the even/odd split b203 predicted, now visible in the ladder itself.* ***
  :1734 
  :1735 ### **THE GATE THAT CAUGHT THE EXECUTOR**
  :1736 
  :1737 G-SONIN — *`F φ` must vanish on `|y| < 1` because `φ` is in the Sonin space, and **nothing in the
  :1738 construction forces it to*** — returned `~1e-15` at `τ = 2π` (pass) and ### **`~1e-3` at
  :1739 `τ = 4π` (halt).** The executor's first diagnosis, an under-refined root, was ### **wrong, and was
  :1740 refuted by its own re-run at a genuine root** (`β = 4.9e-57`). ### **The real cause was the
  :1741 instrument: it assumed `Λ = 1` and was carried to `τ = 4π` anyway** — against the ferry's own
  :1742 clause (b), *"at `τ = 2π` (`Λ = 1`, no rescaling)"*. Corrected to frequency `τ` with the factor `Λ`,
  :1743 the gate passes at `3.7e-19` and the ratio reads `1.0` to `1e-15`.
  :1744 
  :1745 > ***A plausible diagnosis that survives one test is not a diagnosis.***

  :1748 
  :1749 RRT Prop 5 — `V^∓_n = n!(±2iπ)^{−n}U_n` — ### **does not decide either bit, and the reason is
  :1750 structural:** it is a relation between **formal** series coefficients, while `α` and `c` are
  :1751 Borel-sum / Stokes data of the **summed** solutions and of the transform. *A relation among formal
  :1752 coefficients cannot supply them.* It does corroborate one thing b211 and b212 leaned on:
  :1753 `V^−_0 = V^+_0 = 1`, the μ-independence of the leading coefficient, **at source**.

  :1767 ## §25 — WHAT `σ_even` WEIGHTS: THE ROUTE IS CLOSED, AND THE TWO OBJECTS ARE ORTHOGONAL *(added 2026-08-27, b219)*
  :1768 
  :1769 > *** ### **VERDICT: (OTHER).** ### **`σ_even`'s weights are NOT norms of the prolate family on
  :1770 > `S(1,1)`, and the source does not leave it to be inferred — it states that the vectors they
  :1771 > weight are ### ORTHOGONAL TO `S(1,1)`.** ### **The double-name hazard is REALISED, and the two
  :1772 > objects are not merely different: they are orthogonal.** ***
  :1773 >
  :1774 > ### **No computation was run. The read did not license one.**
  :1775 
  :1776 ### **THE DEFINITION, FROM ITS OWNERS**
  :1777 
  :1778 `σ_even = Σ_{n even} t(n) / Σ_n t(n)`, with `t(n) = λ(n)² ξ_n(1)² / (1 − λ(n)²)` (b109's
  :1779 constituent table; the value `0.6165` is b35's). ### **The index `n` is CC's ε-series index, not
  :1780 the prolate index** — b35: *"`ξ_n = √2 · ψ_{2n}` — CC's epsilon-series index `n` names the n-th
  :1781 EVEN prolate,"* so *"even"* in `σ_even` means classical index `2n ≡ 0 mod 4`. ***That is b159's
  :1782 sector split of the constrained class, and not the eigenfunction parity of §22 and §23.***
  :1783 
  :1784 ### **THE SENTENCE THAT DECIDES IT**
  :1785 
  :1786 CC arXiv 2006.13771, quoted at content in `relay/data/b199_archimedean_nonvanishing.txt`:
  :1787 
  :1788 > *"Note that by construction the vectors ### **`ξ_n` are all ORTHOGONAL TO `S(1,1)`** and so are
  :1789 > `η_n = F_eR ξ_n` and `ζ_n`."*
  :1790 
  :1791 ### **TWO INDEPENDENT GROUNDS, WHICH FAIL DIFFERENTLY**
  :1792 
  :1793 | # | ground | what it settles |
  :1794 |:--|:--|:--|
  :1795 | **1** | ### **The family is the wrong family.** RRT **Proposition 7** is an explicit dichotomy: *non-classical ⟹ `ψ(Λ) = ±1`* — ### **with no `λ` at all** — *classical ⟹ `ψ(Λ) = ±√λ`, `0 < λ < 1`*. b35's own check clause C2 records `0 < λ < 1` for every `t(n)` used. | **WHICH FUNCTIONS** |
  :1796 | **2** | ### **`ξ_n(1)²` is a squared POINT VALUE, not a norm.** A norm of the prolate family is `∫₁^∞ ψ² dx` — the right-hand side of §22/§23's identity. ### **No constant converts a point value into an integral across a family.** | **WHICH FUNCTIONAL OF THEM** |
  :1797 
  :1798 *** ### **Ground 2 holds even if ground 1 were set aside** — the weights would not be norms even if
  :1799 the family matched. ***
  :1800 
  :1801 **And the other two candidates, disposed of explicitly.** ### **`|α|²` — NO:** §24 measured
  :1802 `|α| = πΛ` at *every* eigenvalue in *both* families, so `|α|²` is **constant**, and a constant
  :1803 weight makes the share a counting share. ### **`t(n)` spans five orders of magnitude**
  :1804 (`t(0) = 11.9719` against `t(4) = 0.000125459`). ### **`|β′|` — NO:** `β′` is a `μ`-derivative of
  :1805 the non-classical problem's Wronskian determinant; `t(n)` contains neither, and its family is
  :1806 wrong by ground 1.
  :1807 
  :1808 ### **THE CORROBORATIONS, KEPT BELOW THE ARGUMENT ON PURPOSE**
  :1809 
  :1810 The interleaving is strict (§22, §23), so **any** counting share over the non-classical family is
  :1811 exactly `½` — and an `|α|²` weighting would give `½` too. ### **`σ_even = 0.6165 ≠ 0.5`.**
  :1812 ***The value agrees with the verdict. The value did not produce it*** — the brief required the
  :1813 decision to come from the definition, and it did.
  :1814 
  :1815 ### **WHAT WAS FOUND ON THE WAY, AND IS NOT REPAIRED HERE**
  :1816 
  :1817 **(i) The weight formula's citation does not resolve.** The warrant founding the apportionment
  :1818 names *"CC's Lemma 5.4"* and *"Prop 5.5"*. b169: ### **never read at content anywhere in the
  :1819 record.** b171 checked the three named works — `2112.05500` (no such labels), `2310.18423`
  :1820 (neither), `2511.22755` (has a Lemma 5.4, *about truncated matrices and determinants*) — and
  :1821 located the work that **does** carry the symbols, ### **which was not in the bibliography at all:**
  :1822 `arXiv 2006.13771`, Connes & Consani. ***The same paper the corpus already reads for the Sonin
  :1823 space — one paper carrying both objects, which is how two names drift together.*** ### **b219 adds
  :1824 only that this unresolved citation sits DIRECTLY UNDER the weight formula, which raises its
  :1825 priority without discharging it.**
  :1826 
  :1827 **(ii) Two acts quote CC's displayed equation (14) differently** — b176 *verbatim from the PDF*
  :1828 with `λ(n)/√(1−λ(n)²)`, b171 from an HTML rendering with `λ(n)/(1−λ(n)²)`. ### **b219 did not open
  :1829 the PDF and does not settle the published text.** What is worth recording is that
  :1830 `t(n) = [λ/√(1−λ²)]² · ξ_n(1)²`, ### **the square of b176's coefficient** — and the step that would
  :1831 do that squaring is the `ρ → 1⁺` derivative, ***which is precisely the unread "Lemma 5.4."***
  :1832 
  :1833 ### **WHAT THIS SECTION DOES NOT SAY**
  :1834 
  :1835 ### **It does not say `σ_even` is wrong, or the apportionment unsound.** `σ_even` is a correct
  :1836 share **of its own series**; b107's *CLOSED-AT-BENCH, OPEN-AT-DERIVATION* grade is untouched.
  :1837 The finding is about ### **what it is a share OF.**
  :1838 
  :1839 > *** ### **AND THE TEMPTATION IS NAMED, BECAUSE IT WAS REAL.** The instruments to build a
  :1840 > norm-weighted rank table over the non-classical family **exist and are banked** —
  :1841 > `b210_wronskian.py`'s `norm_integral` and `b212_odd.py`'s `norm_integral_parity` compute
  :1842 > `∫₁^∞ ψ² dx` to `1e-16`. ### **A table could have been produced in minutes and would have looked
  :1843 > like an answer.** It would have been a table about a different object than `σ_even`, presented
  :1844 > under `σ_even`'s name — ### **the double-name species itself, committed by the act sent to
  :1845 > diagnose it.** ***The licence clause is what stopped it.*** ***

  :1852 ## §26 — THE AGGREGATION'S FREEDOM: THE CONSTRAINT THAT WOULD PIN IT IS THE CONCLUSION *(added 2026-08-27, b220)*
  :1853 
  :1854 > *** ### **VERDICT: (UNDERDETERMINED).** The constraints the record imposes on term 2's

  :1856 > finite-parameter family. ### **And the reason is sharper than "nobody has written it":
  :1857 > the one constraint that would pin it is `h2` itself, and deriving from it is circular.** ***
  :1858 >
  :1859 > ### **b215 offered "a RULING **or** a RESULT" as two open routes. One of the two is closed.**
  :1860 > ### **No aggregation is adopted, defined, or compiled. File E is untouched.**

  :1862 ### **THE FOUR CONSTRAINTS, WITH OWNERS AND GRADES**
  :1863 
  :1864 | constraint | owner | grade | what it demands of the aggregation |
  :1865 |:--|:--|:--|:--|
  :1866 | **C-TYPE** | File E, **read at source** | exact — it is the file | ### **NOTHING.** `QuotientTrace` carries `value : ℝ` and nothing else |
  :1867 | **C-NORM** | act 7 §2, ### **read at SOURCE for the first time** | proof grade **modulo `ClassRichness`**, whose citation is **UNREAD** | ### **NOT STATED** — the three joints constrain the volume convention, not the assembly |
  :1868 | **C-WEIL** | act 9 §2 / act 7 §2 | ### **NOT AVAILABLE** | — (two independent reasons, below) |

  :1871 ### *** WHY C-WEIL IS NOT AVAILABLE — TWO INDEPENDENT REASONS ***
  :1872 
  :1873 **(1) It has no exact statement.** Act 9 §2 *displays and proves*
  :1874 `τ_q(p,n,k)·p^{k/2} = (pⁿ − p^k)/(pⁿ − 1)` for `1 ≤ k ≤ n−1`, `0` for `k ≥ n`. What it says
  :1875 *about* that result is one sentence: *"The quotient channel's convergence to Weil's coefficients
  :1876 at the level limit: PROOF GRADE longhand."* ### **That sentence names no coefficients, no
  :1877 normalization and no `log p`. It is a LABEL on the theorem, not an exhibited statement.**
  :1878 *(And the range `1 ≤ k ≤ n−1` — the per-place value **vanishes** for `k ≥ n` — has been dropped
  :1879 from the derived line ever since; it is recovered here from source.)*
  :1880 
  :1881 **(2) It is the conclusion.** Act 7 §2 closes: ### ***"Its coefficients against Weil's: part of
  :1882 `(L-identity)`, NOT ASSUMED."*** — and §3 lists that comparison on the equation's **open** side.
  :1883 File E's own docstring: *"STATED, NOT PROVED, NOT CLAIMED … **Its truth at complete roster is
  :1884 `h2`**."* ### **So requiring the aggregate to reproduce Weil's coefficients is requiring
  :1885 `T.value + Q.value = W.wInf − W.wPrimes`, which is `h2`.** An aggregation derived from it is, by
  :1886 construction, the unique one making the identity true — ### **and so can never be evidence FOR
  :1887 the identity.**
  :1888 
  :1889 ### **THE NAMING HAZARD, RESOLVED BEFORE C-WEIL WAS USED**
  :1890 
  :1891 *"Weil's coefficients"* names at least two objects here: ### **(1)** the Weil ledger/criterion
  :1892 (b179, external, File E's `wInf`/`wPrimes`); ### **(2)** the explicit formula's prime-side
  :1893 coefficients (b10's *"weight ~ `p^{−k/2}`, unit `log p` the period"* — graded ### **QUESTION**,
  :1894 *"MATCH-IN-SHAPE / NOT; no promotion either way"*). ### **The record does not say which the
  :1895 convergence line means, and neither reading yields a usable constraint** — (2) for want of a
  :1896 statement, (1) because it is `h2`. *The level limit produces `p^{−k/2}` with no `log p` anywhere.*
  :1897 
  :1898 ### **THE SHADOW — THE CIRCULARITY MADE CHECKABLE**
  :1899 
  :1900 `Core/AggregationCircularityShadow.lean`, ### **9 terminals, 9 of 9 at zero axioms** (Core
  :1901 366 → 375; `AXIOM_PRINTS.txt` still carries **zero** axiom-bearing lines). It shows an
  :1902 aggregation satisfies C-WEIL **iff** it returns the value the identity forces
  :1903 (`cweil_determines`); that such an aggregation always exists for *any* inputs
  :1904 (`cweil_inhabited`); and — the punchline — that the proof the identity then holds ### **is the
  :1905 C-WEIL hypothesis returned unchanged** (`cweil_is_the_assumption`, whose proof term is `:= h`).
  :1906 Negative controls first: `underdetermined_without_cweil` establishes the setup does *not*
  :1907 collapse on its own, so the uniqueness is measuring C-WEIL and not the encoding.
  :1908 ### **It defines no aggregation — `agg` is a variable in every statement.**
  :1909 
  :1910 ### **WHAT IS WANTED NOW**
  :1911 
  :1912 ### **A statement about the built object, INDEPENDENT of the finite-instance identity, that
  :1913 determines how `{ p^{−k(p,cell)/2} }_{p ≤ a²}` combines into one real.** Three candidate
  :1914 sources, ### **each NOT ADOPTED**: **(i)** the restricted-tensor trace, if act 7 §4's
  :1915 *"the unit-normalized restricted-tensor trace"* is made a theorem — ### **the most promising

  :1918 `Q.value` and ***a habit is not a constraint***; **(iii)** an author's **ruling**.

  :2422         ln = math.log(p ** k)
  :2423         if ln <= 2 * L:
  :2424             PR += 2.0 * math.log(p) / math.sqrt(p ** k) * np.interp(ln, vc, corr)
  :2425         k += 1
  :2426 ```
  :2427 
  :2428 ### **That is the Weil explicit formula's prime-side term, written out:**
  :2429 `PR(a) = Σ_p Σ_{k : p^k ≤ a²} (2 log p / p^{k/2}) · corr(log p^k)`.
  :2430 ### **And the cutoff `p^k ≤ a²` is b17's staircase, character for character** — at a **diagonal
  :2431 `a²`** cell. *(Cell species said, per b221: everything here is diagonal; b226's units and act 9's
  :2432 `(p,n,k)` are the other species and are not used.)*
  :2433 
  :2434 **Grades, named and not upgraded by having been found:** `A` is *"the atlas's certified arch
  :2435 column, **BENCH at its pins**"* and *"an **INPUT** to b38's definition … never an output"*; `PR`'s
  :2436 only definition is the code. ### **Neither is a derivation. Both are instrument arithmetic.**
  :2437 
  :2438 ### *** THE SPACES — THE ACT'S CENTRAL STRUCTURAL POINT ***
  :2439 
  :2440 `A` is a **test-function integral** against a kernel. `PR` lives on ### **nothing — it is an
  :2441 arithmetic sum** over prime powers.
  :2442 
  :2443 > ### **So the identity puts TRACES on the left and an ARITHMETIC SUM on the right.** Asking whether
  :2444 > the quotient channel "equals `wPrimes`" is asking whether a trace equals a prime sum —
  :2445 > ### **which is `(L-identity)` itself, the undecided thing.**
  :2446 > *b227 found the operator and the state on different spaces; this act finds the right-hand side on
  :2447 > **no space at all**. **The two sides are not two traces; they are a trace and a sum.***
  :2448 
  :2449 **And a sharpening of b220**, which searched for exactly this: b220 reported act 9's convergence
  :2450 line names *"**no `log p`**"*. ### **The `log p` is here, in `PR`, and not in act 9's `τ_q`.** Act
  :2451 9's limit gives `p^{−k/2}`; `PR`'s weight is `2 log p · p^{−k/2}`. ### **The `p^{−k/2}` matches; the
  :2452 `2 log p` is the difference** — precisely b10's *"Lefschetz wonder"* comparison, which b10 graded
  :2453 **QUESTION** with *"no promotion either way."* ***This act does not promote it either.***
  :2454 
  :2455 ### **THE MISSING SENTENCE:** the record has no **stated** definition — it has code that computes
  :2456 one. The step is to promote the instrument's arithmetic to a statement, with its test function,
  :2457 cutoff `L` and normalization fixed. ### **It wants a RESULT or a RULING; it does not want a read —
  :2458 the read is now done.**

  :2657 ## §34 — THE SIGN OF `A`, DERIVED; AND **THE IMPORT LEDGER**, OPENED *(added 2026-08-28, b232)*
  :2658 
  :2659 ### §34.1 — the verdict
  :2660 
  :2661 ### **(FORCED): `wInf = −A`.** Derived by citation from Connes–Consani's own equation (1), **not**
  :2662 from any comparison coming out.
  :2663 
  :2664 **CC's equation (1), verbatim as displayed** (arXiv:2006.13771, "Weil positivity and Trace formula:
  :2665 the archimedean place", read at content via ar5iv 2026-08-28):
  :2666 
  :2667 > **f~(0) − Σ_{ρ∈Z} f~(ρ) + f~(1) = Σ_v 𝒲_v(f)**,  **f~(s) := ∫_0^∞ f(x) x^{s−1} dx**
  :2668 
  :2669 with, before it, *"It was shown by A. Weil that the Riemann Hypothesis (RH) is equivalent to the
  :2670 negativity of the right-hand side of the Riemann-Weil explicit formula"*; after it, *"where Z is the
  :2671 multi-set of non-trivial zeros of the Riemann zeta function"*; and, fixing where the primes live,
  :2672 *"**The key point being that the right-hand side of the explicit formula, when evaluated on a test
  :2673 function f with compact support, involves only finitely many primes.**"*
  :2674 
  :2675 ### **All three sides are fixed by CC's own sentences** — zero side on the left, places side (with
  :2676 the primes) on the right, `𝒲_∞` one member of that right-hand sum (eq. 53: **𝒲_∞(f) = −∫ f(ρ^{−1})
  :2677 τ(ρ) d*ρ**).
  :2678 
  :2679 **The derivation, four steps:** rearrange (1) to `Σ_ρ f~(ρ) = [poles] − Σ_v 𝒲_v(f)`; set beside the
  :2680 instrument's own committed docstring arrangement `Σ_γ ĥ(γ) = ĥ(i/2) + ĥ(−i/2) − PRIME + ARCH`
  :2681 (quoted in `SIGN_ARRANGEMENT_RECONCILIATION` §1 as *"committed before any answer"* — **but see the
  :2682 b233 amendment at §34.5: the docstring itself appends `[sign fixed BY the E2 calibration]`, which
  :2683 that quotation does not carry**); match zeros to
  :2684 zeros and poles to poles, leaving `Σ_v 𝒲_v = PR − A`; split by CC's own sentence into `Σ_p 𝒲_p =
  :2685 +PR` and ### **`𝒲_∞ = −A`**. Since File E binds `wInf` to `𝒲_∞` *in the CC convention* by name,
  :2686 ### **`wInf = −A`.**
  :2687 
  :2688 **The named sub-assumption, disclosed:** CC's finite-place formula is **not in the retrieved text**

  :2690 licensed by CC's sentence, not a quoted display.

  :2695 `𝒲_∞ ≥ 0 > A` on the same class: ### **`+A` is excluded, `−A` is consistent.**

  :2754 while CC's places-sum derived above is **`Σ_v 𝒲_v = −A + PR`**. ### **They differ in the prime
  :2755 term's sign, so File E's `wInf − wPrimes` is not CC's `Σ_v 𝒲_v`.** That may be intended — File E
  :2756 states its own relation — **but it is a second convention question and this act does not decide it.**

  :2760 ## §35 — **THE IMPORT LEDGER** *(opened 2026-08-28, b232)*
  :2761 
  :2762 > ### **IMPORTS ARE ENUMERATED LIKE AXIOMS AND NEVER WEAR THE CORPUS'S GRADE.**
  :2763 > *An import is a statement the corpus uses but did not derive and does not own. It is listed here
  :2764 > with its citation and what rests on it, so that what the programme has borrowed is as legible as
  :2765 > what it has proved. Nothing in this section is a result of this corpus.*
  :2766 
  :2767 **IMP-1 — the two-ended classical prime sum.**
  :2768 *Statement:* `Σ_n Λ(n) n^(−1/2) [ g(log n) + g(−log n) ]` — the classical explicit formula's prime
  :2769 side in two-ended form.
  :2770 *Citation:* A. Weil, *"Sur les 'formules explicites' de la théorie des nombres premiers"* (1952);
  :2771 carried as [33] in CC's own bibliography (b179, read at content).
  :2772 *What rests on it:* b231's fold — for a summand even in the signed index,
  :2773 `Σ_{k∈S}[T(k) + T(−k)] = 2·Σ_{k∈S} T(k)` — hence the reading that the adopted `wPrimes`'s factor
  :2774 `2` is the folded mirror term. The fold is compiled (`FoldedMirrorShadow`, 14 terminals, zero
  :2775 axioms).
  :2776 *Status:* ### **b231's verdict was (PARTIAL) precisely because this is an import — no owner in the
  :2777 four trees writes the two-ended sum.** The algebra is owned; **the provenance is not.**
  :2778 
  :2779 **IMP-2 — CC's explicit-formula arrangement.**
  :2780 *Statement:* `f~(0) − Σ_{ρ∈Z} f~(ρ) + f~(1) = Σ_v 𝒲_v(f)`, with the primes on the right-hand side
  :2781 and `𝒲_∞` one member of that sum.
  :2782 *Citation:* arXiv:2006.13771, Connes & Consani, *"Weil positivity and Trace formula: the
  :2783 archimedean place"*, equation (1) with its adjacent sentences; read at content via ar5iv,
  :2784 2026-08-28.
  :2785 *What rests on it:* §34's derivation of `wInf = −A`, and therefore the completeness of the ledger's
  :2786 cell-level statement.
  :2787 *Status:* ### **derived-by-citation; the citation is the load. It does not become the corpus's
  :2788 result by being used.**
  :2789 *Named sub-assumption:* the finite-place term carries `+PR` — **CC's finite-place formula is not in
  :2790 the retrieved text**; the step is a term-class matching.

  :2914 
  :2915 | import | statement | verification | work-order |
  :2916 |:--|:--|:--|:--|
  :2917 | **IMP-1** | the two-ended classical prime sum (Weil 1952; CC's own [33]) | ### **TRUSTED-AT-CITE** — *put to the bench at b233 and **the registered pass-criterion was not met**; see below* | ### `W-ORD-IMP1-BUDGET` |
  :2918 | **IMP-2** | CC's explicit-formula arrangement (2006.13771 eq. 1) | ### **TRUSTED-AT-CITE** — *its load is a **labelling**, which no residual can reach* | ### `W-ORD-IMP2-TAU` |
  :2919 
  :2920 **IMP-1 — why it was not promoted.** b233 computed CC's equation (1) on both sides at the corpus's
  :2921 own bump, at the **diagonal a² cells 2, 3, 4**: the zero side from `mpmath.zetazero` (N = 1000,
  :2922 fresh, cross-checked against the banked ordinates — control passed at 0.000e+00), the places side
  :2923 from `b38_act10.left_side` **imported unmodified**, under five axis settings. ### **The two sides
  :2924 agree to ~2e−8 relative — and the act's registered criterion was that the residual be bounded by
  :2925 the zero-truncation tail, which it is not, at any cell or any axis (15 of 15 above the bound).**
  :2926 The residual is invariant under the zero truncation and falls with the places-side grid; the
  :2927 b233 diagnostic puts its order between first and second, **refuting its own clean-`(Δv)²`
  :2928 hypothesis while confirming the direction**.
  :2929 
  :2930 > ### **The grade follows the criterion, not the impression. A criterion rewritten after the
  :2931 > numbers is not a criterion, and the one move that would have delivered a clean
  :2932 > VERIFIED-AT-BENCH — replacing the tail bound with a budget fitted to what came out — is the move
  :2933 > this programme exists to refuse. No axis was tuned and the bar was not moved.**
  :2934 
  :2935 ### **`W-ORD-IMP1-BUDGET` — DISCHARGED 2026-08-28 (b238), AND IMP-1 STAYS TRUSTED-AT-CITE.**
  :2936 The budget was built and every source priced by measurement. ### **Four of the five came out at zero:** `A`, `P` and `Z` carry no grid error (machine epsilon); the zero truncation is invariant over a factor of 4 in `N`; the zeros at doubled precision agree to `0.000e+00`; and ### **b234's registered half-order edge hypothesis is REFUTED by direct measurement** — the trapezoid on `∫_{−1}^{1} exp(−1/(1−t²)) dt` matches `mpmath.quad` to `0.000e+00` at every grid from 2001 to 32001 (Euler–Maclaurin: the bump's flat endpoints cost nothing). ### **What remains is `np.interp` in the prime column plus a `1.2e−13` float floor, and it explains ~97% of every observed residual.**
  :2937 
  :2938 > ### **THE CRITERION WAS DERIVED FROM THAT BUDGET AND BANKED BEFORE THE FINAL RUN** — fit on `NV ∈ {2001, 8001, 16001}`, tested out-of-sample at `{4001, 6001}`, the two files' timestamps 65 seconds apart on disk and gated. ### **FIVE CELLS OF SIX CAME IN WITHIN THEIR BOUND; ONE DID NOT** — `a²=3` at `NV=6001`, `2.218e−08` against `2.133e−08`, ### **over by a factor of 1.04.** The banked pass rule said *"one cell over the bound is branch (HELD)"*, so ### **it is HELD.**
  :2939 >
  :2940 > ### **The cause is not an unexplained physical effect: it is an UNDER-SAMPLED ENVELOPE.** `K` was taken as the maximum over **three** samples of a quantity the measurement showed to **jitter** — because the evaluation points `log p^k` are fixed while the grid refines, so an order is not well defined for a fixed point on a refining grid. ### **A maximum over three samples of a jittering quantity is not an envelope; it is three samples.**
  :2941 >
  :2942 > ### **Raising `K` by 4% would have delivered (PROMOTED) at all six cells. It was not raised.** *b233 failed a criterion that was wrong; b238 failed a criterion that was nearly right, and the second refusal is the harder one.*
  :2943 
  :2944 ### **NEW WORK-ORDER `W-ORD-IMP1-ENVELOPE`:** re-derive `K` as a **true envelope over the jitter** — sample many `NV` and take the supremum, or **bound `|corr''|` directly and use the analytic `h²/8` constant**, which removes the jitter from the estimate entirely. ### **Then the criterion is a bound rather than a sample.** Tool-reachable; not run.
  :2945 
  :2946 *And what (HELD) does not mean: the two sides agree to ~2e−8 relative at `a²=3` and 5e−9 at `a²=4`. **That is agreement. What is held is a GRADE.***
  :2947 
  :2948 `W-ORD-IMP1-BUDGET`: re-run with a **correct error budget registered in advance** — the places-side
  :2949 quadrature order established first, the zero tail kept as a separate and negligible term, and the
  :2950 pass-criterion set to that budget **before the first number**. Tool-reachable; not run.
  :2951 
  :2952 `W-ORD-IMP2-TAU`: CC displays its own archimedean term at eq. (53) as `𝒲_∞(f) = −∫ f(ρ^{−1}) τ(ρ)
  :2953 d*ρ`, with (39) `τ(ρ) = ρ^{1/2}/2 · (1/(1+ρ) + 1/|1−ρ|)`. ### **Compute that integral — principal
  :2954 value at ρ = 1 — at the banked diagonal a² cells and compare it to `−A`.** That would move IMP-2
  :2955 from a matching argument to an internal verification of the identification §34 derived.
  :2956 Tool-reachable; not run.
  :2957 
  :2958 *Bank: `relay/data/b233_the_arrangement.txt`. **The bar's first use produced a non-promotion.

  :2963 ### §36 — THE ARRANGEMENT: **ROUTED, ONE RULING FROM COMPLETE** *(added 2026-08-28, b233)*
  :2964 
  :2965 **The question.** With both columns defined — `wPrimes = PR` (b229) and `wInf = −A` (b232) — File
  :2966 E's right side is `−A − PR`, while CC's places-sum is `Σ_v 𝒲_v = −A + PR`. They differ in the prime
  :2967 term's sign.
  :2968 
  :2969 **File E's three texts, and what they do not contain.** The relation
  :2970 `T.value + Q.value = W.wInf - W.wPrimes`; the field docstring *"`W_∞` (**CC convention**, the act-12
  :2971 dictionary) **and the prime sum**"*; the header line *"the atlas's certified columns … **in the CC
  :2972 sign convention**"*. ### **File E names its fields and writes its operator. It nowhere states the
  :2973 operator's intent.**
  :2974 
  :2975 **The three candidates, tested.**
  :2976 - **(i) the minus is File E's own convention, knowingly differing from CC's** — requires a
  :2977   quotation saying so. Searched at content: File E whole; the chain's every `wInf`/`wPrimes` line;
  :2978   **every occurrence of `wPrimes` in `relay/reports` (15 lines, 8 files)**; the acts narrative.
  :2979   ### **(ABSENT).** *Positive control: those same searches returned all 15 report lines and File
  :2980   E's own three texts — the search could say yes.*
  :2981 - **(ii) File E intends CC's sum** — requires File E's **text** to license `wPrimes = −PR` or
  :2982   `wInf + wPrimes`. ### **It licenses neither**: it writes a minus and calls the field "the prime
  :2983   sum". The strongest evidence for (ii) is an owner's sentence that is **not a licence** — the acts
  :2984   narrative §25(c): *"L1+L2+L3 assemble to `object ≤ W_∞ + Σ_p W_p` (**the primes enter
  :2985   POSITIVELY** via L3) — **not the dictated `W_∞ − Σ_p W_p`**"*. ### **That is evidence about the
  :2986   mathematics, not a licence to rewrite a stated object.** *(Its second half — "fails numerically
  :2987   in BOTH conventions" — is a comparison coming out and was **not consulted**.)*
  :2988 - **(iii) the texts underdetermine it** — ### **the verdict.**
  :2989 
  :2990 **The diagnosis, stated and not adopted.** File E's minus is exactly right under **Day-1's**
  :2991 convention (`wInf − wPrimes = A − PR`, the corpus's own `LEFT` column, computed under that name in
  :2992 the narrative's §20(a)) and names **no object the corpus computes** under CC's (`−A − PR`). §34
  :2993 established that the corpus carries two `W_∞`s; ### **b233's finding is that File E may carry one
  :2994 of each.** *A diagnosis is not a settlement, and an executor does not choose between an author's
  :2995 docstring and an author's operator.*
  :2996 
  :2997 **The ruling asked for, with its three outcomes and no recommendation:**
  :2998 
  :2999 | | if it governs | consequence |
  :3000 |:--|:--|:--|
  :3001 | **(A)** | the **docstring** | `wInf = −A` stands, the operator becomes `+`: `T + Q = −A + PR = Σ_v 𝒲_v`. **File E's relation is edited; b232's adoption stands.** |
  :3002 | **(B)** | the **operator** | the minus stands and `wInf` is Day-1's `+A`: `T + Q = A − PR`, the corpus's own `LEFT`. **File E's docstring is edited; b232's sign remains true of CC's `𝒲_∞` but ceases to bind `wInf`.** |
  :3003 | **(C)** | **both, as written** | the right side is `−A − PR`, an object the corpus does not compute, stated deliberately — and it should then be **said in File E** so no reader reconstructs it. |
  :3004 
  :3005 ### **The identity's statement is therefore ONE-RULING-FROM-COMPLETE: both columns defined, the

  :3031 ### §37.1 — the mapping, one register, every step a quotation or a citation
  :3032

  :3047 
  :3048 ### §37.2 — the demarcation *(program-voice)*: "isn't this just Weil positivity?"
  :3049 
  :3050 **[IMPORT]** What classical Weil positivity states, quoted at content by b179 from CC's own eq. (2):
  :3051 *"**RH ⟺ Σ_v 𝒲_v(g ∗ ḡ^♯) ≤ 0**, for all g in C_c^∞(ℝ*_+) with g̃(z) = 0 for all z in F"* — which CC
  :3052 **credit to A. Weil [33], following Yoshida [34]**. ### **It is an equivalence, it is classical, and

  :32 theorem radical_zero (F : H →ₗᵢ[ℂ] H) (S : Submodule ℂ H)
  :33     (hsurj : ∀ z ∈ S, ∃ y ∈ S, F y = z) (x : H) (hx : x ∈ S)
  :34     (h : ∀ y ∈ S, ⟪x, F y⟫_ℂ = 0) : x = 0 := by
  :35   obtain ⟨y, hy, hFy⟩ := hsurj x hx

  :40 /-- THEOREM (R2-1, the CNU theorem's abstract engine): a compressed isometry with the
  :41     ESCAPE property (no nonzero vector of `S` has its whole forward orbit in `S`) has no
  :42     unimodular eigenvalue: if the compression of `U` to `S` has `P(Ux) = λ x` with
  :43     `|λ| = 1`, then `x = 0`. The escape hypothesis is exactly what the p-adic Fourier
  :44     computation discharges (banked exact witnesses); here it is a hypothesis. PROVED
  :45     from it, no sorry. -/
  :46 theorem no_unimodular_eigenvalue [CompleteSpace H]
  :47     (U : H ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] H) (S : Submodule ℂ H) [S.HasOrthogonalProjection]
  :48     (hesc : ∀ x ∈ S, (∀ k : ℕ, (fun y => U y)^[k] x ∈ S) → x = 0)
  :49     (x : H) (hx : x ∈ S) (lam : ℂ) (hlam : ‖lam‖ = 1)
  :50     (heig : S.starProjection (U x) = lam • x) : x = 0 := by
  :51   have hUxnorm : ‖S.starProjection (U x)‖ = ‖U x‖ := by
  :52     rw [heig, norm_smul, hlam, one_mul, U.norm_map]
  :53   have hUxmem : U x ∈ S := (S.mem_iff_norm_starProjection (U x)).mpr hUxnorm
  :54   have hUx : U x = lam • x := by
  :55     rw [← Submodule.starProjection_eq_self_iff.mpr hUxmem, heig]
  :56   have horbit : ∀ k : ℕ, (fun y => U y)^[k] x = lam ^ k • x := by
  :57     intro k
  :58     induction k with
  :59     | zero => simp
  :60     | succ n ih =>
  :61       rw [Function.iterate_succ_apply', ih, map_smul, hUx, smul_smul, pow_succ, mul_comm]
  :62   refine hesc x hx fun k => ?_
  :63   rw [horbit k]
  :64   exact S.smul_mem _ hx

  :66 /-- the `C₄` idempotent attached to a fourth root `lam` (no inverses appear: `lam⁻¹ =
  :67     lam³` when `lam⁴ = 1`). -/
  :68 noncomputable def proj4 (F : H →ₗ[ℂ] H) (lam : ℂ) (x : H) : H :=
  :69   (4 : ℂ)⁻¹ • (x + lam ^ 3 • F x + lam ^ 2 • F (F x) + lam • F (F (F x)))
  :70 
  :71 /-- THE FOUR-SECTOR DECOMPOSITION, CLAUSE ONE (the eigen-property), PROVED — the
  :72     former `four_sector_decomposition_stub`'s first half, discharged 2026-08-22
  :73     (b100, the sense build act): for a finite-order `F` (`F⁴ = 1`) and any fourth
  :74     root `lam`, the `C₄` idempotent lands in `ker(F − lam)`. Closed-form spectral
  :75     projections; no sorry. -/
  :76 theorem proj4_eigen (F : H →ₗ[ℂ] H) (h4 : ∀ y : H, F (F (F (F y))) = y)
  :77     (lam : ℂ) (hlam : lam ^ 4 = 1) (x : H) :
  :78     F (proj4 F lam x) = lam • proj4 F lam x := by
  :79   unfold proj4
  :80   rw [map_smul, smul_comm]
  :81   congr 1
  :82   rw [map_add, map_add, map_add, map_smul, map_smul, map_smul, h4]
  :83   rw [smul_add, smul_add, smul_add, smul_smul, smul_smul, smul_smul]
  :84   rw [show lam * lam ^ 3 = lam ^ 4 by ring, hlam, one_smul]
  :85   rw [show lam * lam ^ 2 = lam ^ 3 by ring, show lam * lam = lam ^ 2 by ring]
  :86   abel
  :87 
  :88 /-- THE FOUR-SECTOR DECOMPOSITION, CLAUSE TWO (completeness), PROVED — the four
  :89     `C₄` idempotents sum to the identity, so `H = ⊕_{lam⁴=1} ker(F − lam)` as the
  :90     docstring stated. With `proj4_eigen`: the decomposition, discharged. No sorry. -/
  :91 theorem proj4_sum (F : H →ₗ[ℂ] H) (x : H) :
  :92     proj4 F 1 x + proj4 F Complex.I x + proj4 F (-1) x + proj4 F (-Complex.I) x = x := by
  :93   unfold proj4
  :94   have hI2 : Complex.I ^ 2 = -1 := Complex.I_sq
  :95   have hI3 : Complex.I ^ 3 = -Complex.I := by rw [pow_succ, hI2]; ring
  :96   have hnI2 : (-Complex.I) ^ 2 = -1 := by rw [neg_pow]; simp [hI2]
  :97   have hnI3 : (-Complex.I) ^ 3 = Complex.I := by rw [pow_succ, hnI2]; ring
  :98   rw [hI2, hI3, hnI2, hnI3]
  :99   norm_num
  :100   module

  :102 /-- THE SOT LIMIT OF THE LEVEL COMPRESSIONS, PROVED — the former stub's second
  :103     half, discharged 2026-08-22 (b100): for a MONOTONE family `U` of submodules
  :104     with orthogonal projections, the compressions of a bounded operator `A` to the
  :105     levels converge strongly to the compression on the closure of the supremum.
  :106     Mathlib's `starProjection_tendsto_closure_iSup` supplies the projection
  :107     convergence; the operator step is the three-term split with the `‖P‖ ≤ 1`
  :108     domination. No sorry.

  :110     ### THE SCOPE, STATED SO NO READER OVER-CLAIMS IT (b100, filed at full

  :113     programme's concrete level family satisfies the MONOTONICITY hypothesis; that
  :114     is a separate, concrete question whose owner is the construction, and b70's
  :115     decided non-stabilization witness bears on it. The abstract lemma is proved;

  :117 theorem compression_tendsto {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι]
  :118     (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) [∀ i, (U i).HasOrthogonalProjection]
  :119     [(⨆ i, U i).topologicalClosure.HasOrthogonalProjection]
  :120     (hU : Monotone U) (A : H →L[ℂ] H) (x : H) :
  :121     Filter.Tendsto (fun i => (U i).starProjection (A ((U i).starProjection x))) Filter.atTop
  :122       (nhds ((⨆ i, U i).topologicalClosure.starProjection
  :123             (A ((⨆ i, U i).topologicalClosure.starProjection x)))) := by
  :124   set P := (⨆ i, U i).topologicalClosure with hP
  :125   have h2 : Filter.Tendsto (fun i => (U i).starProjection (A (P.starProjection x)))
  :126       Filter.atTop (nhds (P.starProjection (A (P.starProjection x)))) :=
  :127     Submodule.starProjection_tendsto_closure_iSup U hU _
  :128   have hx : Filter.Tendsto (fun i => (U i).starProjection x) Filter.atTop
  :129       (nhds (P.starProjection x)) :=
  :130     Submodule.starProjection_tendsto_closure_iSup U hU x
  :131   have hxn : Filter.Tendsto (fun i => ‖(U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x‖)
  :132       Filter.atTop (nhds 0) := tendsto_iff_norm_sub_tendsto_zero.mp hx
  :133   have h1 : Filter.Tendsto (fun i => (U i).starProjection (A ((U i).starProjection x))
  :134       - (U i).starProjection (A (P.starProjection x))) Filter.atTop (nhds 0) := by
  :135     rw [tendsto_zero_iff_norm_tendsto_zero]
  :136     refine squeeze_zero (g := fun i => ‖A‖ * ‖(U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x‖)
  :137       (fun i => norm_nonneg _) (fun i => ?_) ?_
  :138     · have hlin : (U i).starProjection (A ((U i).starProjection x))
  :139           - (U i).starProjection (A (P.starProjection x))
  :140           = (U i).starProjection (A ((U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x)) := by
  :141         rw [← map_sub, ← map_sub]
  :142       rw [hlin]
  :143       calc ‖(U i).starProjection (A ((U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x))‖
  :144           ≤ ‖A ((U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x)‖ :=
  :145             Submodule.norm_starProjection_apply_le _ _
  :146         _ ≤ ‖A‖ * ‖(U i).starProjection x - P.starProjection x‖ := A.le_opNorm _
  :147     · have := hxn.const_mul ‖A‖
  :148       simpa using this
  :149   have hsum := h1.add h2
  :150   simpa using hsum

  :152 /-- THE HULL (b101, the monotonicity act): the running span of an arbitrary
  :153     family — constructed unconditionally, so that a limit object exists for ANY
  :154     level family whatever, monotone or not. -/
  :155 def hull {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι] (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) (i : ι) : Submodule ℂ H :=
  :156   ⨆ j, ⨆ (_ : j ≤ i), U j
  :157 
  :158 theorem hullMono {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι] (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) :
  :159     Monotone (hull U) := by
  :160   intro a b hab
  :161   refine iSup_mono' fun j => ⟨j, ?_⟩
  :162   exact iSup_mono' fun hj => ⟨le_trans hj hab, le_rfl⟩
  :163 
  :164 theorem hull_ge {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι] (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) (i : ι) :
  :165     U i ≤ hull U i :=
  :166   le_iSup_of_le i (le_iSup_of_le le_rfl le_rfl)
  :167 
  :168 theorem hull_iSup_eq {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι] (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) :
  :169     (⨆ i, hull U i) = ⨆ i, U i := by
  :170   apply le_antisymm
  :171   · exact iSup_le fun i => iSup_le fun j => iSup_le fun _ => le_iSup U j
  :172   · exact iSup_mono fun i => hull_ge U i
  :173 
  :174 /-- THE HULL'S SOT LIMIT, UNCONDITIONAL IN THE FAMILY: no monotonicity
  :175     hypothesis on `U` -- the hull supplies it by construction. The limit object is
  :176     the compression on the closure of the hull's supremum, and `hull_iSup_eq`
  :177     (proved above) identifies that supremum with the ORIGINAL family's. -/
  :178 theorem hull_compression_tendsto {ι : Type*} [Preorder ι]
  :179     (U : ι → Submodule ℂ H) [∀ i, (hull U i).HasOrthogonalProjection]
  :180     [(⨆ i, hull U i).topologicalClosure.HasOrthogonalProjection]
  :181     (A : H →L[ℂ] H) (x : H) :
  :182     Filter.Tendsto (fun i => (hull U i).starProjection (A ((hull U i).starProjection x))) Filter.atTop
  :183       (nhds ((⨆ i, hull U i).topologicalClosure.starProjection
  :184             (A ((⨆ i, hull U i).topologicalClosure.starProjection x)))) :=
  :185   compression_tendsto (hull U) (hullMono U) A x

  :187 /-- ACT 6 (the ε-lemma's clean half): eigenvectors of `F` stay eigenvectors under any
  :188     commuting operator — if `A∘F = F∘A` and `F x = λ•x` then `F (A x) = λ•(A x)`.
  :189     THE USE: on Weil-even test data `ϑ(g)` commutes with `F` (the intertwining
  :190     `F U_λ = U_{1/λ} F` plus `g(λ) = g(1/λ)`), so the `±1` sectors of the even Sonin
  :191     space are `ϑ(g)`-invariant and the trace SPLITS: our `Θ` is CC Thm 4.7's trace
  :192     MINUS the `(−1)`-sector term. The trace-class bookkeeping of that split is OWNED
  :193     by the ε-lemma (build document §14). PROVED, no sorry. -/
  :194 theorem eigenvector_of_commute (A F : H →ₗ[ℂ] H) (hc : ∀ x, A (F x) = F (A x))
  :195     (lam : ℂ) (x : H) (hx : F x = lam • x) : F (A x) = lam • A x := by
  :196   rw [← hc, hx, map_smul]

  :198 /-- ACT 12 (the inequality's L2 link, PROVED): for nested closed subspaces `T ≤ S`
  :199     the compressed positive form is dominated — `‖P_T x‖ ≤ ‖P_S x‖`. With `T` the
  :200     `E₁` sector (`Π₊S`, sitting 22's decomposition) inside `S` the Sonin space, this
  :201     is "the `E₁` positive form ≤ the full one": the chain's sound archimedean link.
  :202     PROVED, no sorry. -/
  :203 theorem nested_projection_norm_le [CompleteSpace H] (T S : Submodule ℂ H)
  :204     [T.HasOrthogonalProjection] [S.HasOrthogonalProjection] (hTS : T ≤ S) (x : H) :
  :205     ‖T.starProjection x‖ ≤ ‖S.starProjection x‖ := by
  :206   have h := Submodule.starProjection_comp_starProjection_of_le (𝕜 := ℂ) hTS
  :207   have hx : T.starProjection (S.starProjection x) = T.starProjection x := by
  :208     simpa using DFunLike.congr_fun h x
  :209   calc ‖T.starProjection x‖ = ‖T.starProjection (S.starProjection x)‖ := by rw [hx]
  :210     _ ≤ ‖S.starProjection x‖ := T.norm_starProjection_apply_le _
  :211 
  :212 /-- ACT 14 (the no-exact-level obstruction, PROVED): ℝ has no compact open additive
  :213     subgroup — an open subgroup of the connected line is clopen, hence everything,
  :214     and the line is noncompact. This is exactly why sitting 8's exact-transport chart
  :215     (which rides ℚ_p's compact-open levels `ℤ_p ⊃ p^nℤ_p`) cannot transpose to `∞`:
  :216     the archimedean place is the place WITHOUT finite exact levels — the grid model
  :217     is an approximation scheme, never a restriction. PROVED, no sorry. -/
  :218 theorem real_no_compact_open_addSubgroup (H : AddSubgroup ℝ)
  :219     (ho : IsOpen (H : Set ℝ)) : ¬ IsCompact (H : Set ℝ) := by
  :220   have hclop : IsClopen (H : Set ℝ) := (⟨H, ho⟩ : OpenAddSubgroup ℝ).isClopen
  :221   have huniv : (H : Set ℝ) = Set.univ := hclop.eq_univ ⟨0, H.zero_mem⟩
  :222   intro hc
  :223   rw [huniv] at hc
  :224   exact noncompact_univ ℝ hc

  :226 /-- THE NEXT ERA's OPENING THEOREM (theorem-first; the finite places closed at EVERY p):
  :227     the fixed-point localization, GENERAL p — for any prime `p`, level `n ≥ 1`, step
  :228     `k ≥ 1`, NO Sonin cell `1 ≤ α < p^n` satisfies `p^n ∣ α(p^k − 1)`. This is the
  :229     general-`p` core of trace silence and of the escape hypothesis' arithmetic: the
  :230     banked `p = 2, 3` instances (TraceSilence, no axioms) are its shadows; every other
  :231     step of sitting 20's chain is p-free by construction (the abstract theorems above;
  :232     file A proved for arbitrary p). PROVED, no sorry. -/
  :233 theorem general_p_no_fixed_cell (p n k α : ℕ) (hp : p.Prime)
  :234     (hn : 1 ≤ n) (hk : 1 ≤ k) (hα1 : 1 ≤ α) (hαn : α < p ^ n) :
  :235     ¬ (p ^ n ∣ α * (p ^ k - 1)) := by
  :236   intro hdvd
  :237   have hpk : 1 ≤ p ^ k := Nat.one_le_pow _ _ hp.pos
  :238   have hndvd : ¬ (p ∣ p ^ k - 1) := by
  :239     intro h
  :240     have h2 : p ∣ p ^ k := dvd_pow_self p (Nat.one_le_iff_ne_zero.mp hk)
  :241     have h3 : p ∣ 1 := by
  :242       have h4 := Nat.dvd_sub h2 h
  :243       rwa [Nat.sub_sub_self hpk] at h4
  :244     exact hp.one_lt.not_ge (Nat.le_of_dvd one_pos h3)
  :245   have hcop : Nat.Coprime (p ^ n) (p ^ k - 1) :=
  :246     Nat.Coprime.pow_left _ ((Nat.Prime.coprime_iff_not_dvd hp).mpr hndvd)
  :247   have hα : p ^ n ∣ α := (Nat.Coprime.dvd_of_dvd_mul_right hcop) hdvd
  :248   exact (Nat.le_of_dvd (Nat.lt_of_lt_of_le Nat.zero_lt_one hα1) hα).not_gt hαn
  :249 
  :250 
  :251 /-! ### THE TOWER'S ALL-LEVELS ARITHMETIC (b102, the eighth seam close's strikeable)
  :252 
  :253   The four index facts that row 65 decided at the banked truncation pairs, here
  :254   PROVED FOR ALL `p` and `n` (`0 < p`; the banked cells are prime powers, so the
  :255   hypothesis is free there). The chain is the one the b101 registration wrote
  :256   longhand; what changes is the grade.
  :257 
  :258   ### WHY THIS LIVES AT THE CLASSICAL PROFILE AND NOT AT THE CORE ZERO-AXIOM BAR
  :259   (the strikeable's honest landing, verified by print this act): the statements
  :260   are PROVED, but they cannot reach the zero-axiom bar, and the reason is a
  :261   property of Lean's own library rather than of the mathematics. Every route to
  :262   them runs through core `Nat` lemmas that are themselves propext-carrying —
  :263   `Nat.mul_assoc`, `Nat.pow_add`, `Nat.dvd_of_mod_eq_zero`, `Nat.mul_mod_mul_left`,
  :264   `Nat.add_mul_mod_self_left`, `Nat.mul_div_cancel_left`, `Nat.mul_mod_right`,
  :265   `Nat.mul_left_comm` (each printed this act). `rw` itself is clean; the taint is
  :266   inherited transitively, not introduced by the proofs. Reaching the bar would
  :267   mean reproving a chunk of core `Nat` arithmetic, which is not this act's
  :268   business. So: the banked-pairs terminals stay in Core at the zero-axiom bar
  :269   (row 65, unchanged), and the universal statements live here, declared, at
  :270   {propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound}. `simp`, `omega` and `ac_rfl` are not
  :271   used anywhere below.
  :272 -/
  :273 
  :274 /-- exponent split: p^(2n+2) = p^(n+1) * p^(n+1) -/
  :275 theorem pow_split_even (p n : Nat) : p ^ (2 * n + 2) = p ^ (n + 1) * p ^ (n + 1) := by
  :276   rw [← Nat.pow_add]
  :277   have : n + 1 + (n + 1) = 2 * n + 2 := by
  :278     rw [Nat.two_mul, Nat.add_assoc, Nat.add_comm 1 (n + 1), Nat.add_assoc]
  :279     show n + (n + 2) = n + n + 2
  :280     exact (Nat.add_assoc n n 2).symm
  :281   rw [this]
  :282 
  :283 /-- exponent split: p^(2n+1) = p^(n+1) * p^n -/
  :284 theorem pow_split_odd (p n : Nat) : p ^ (2 * n + 1) = p ^ (n + 1) * p ^ n := by
  :285   rw [← Nat.pow_add]
  :286   have : n + 1 + n = 2 * n + 1 := by
  :287     rw [Nat.two_mul, Nat.add_right_comm]
  :288   rw [this]
  :289 
  :290 /-- (2) THE INNER-SUM RULE, ALL LEVELS -/
  :291 theorem inner_sum_general (p n r : Nat) (h : r % p ^ (n + 1) = 0) :
  :292     (r * p ^ (2 * n + 1)) % p ^ (2 * n + 2) = 0 := by
  :293   obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ := Nat.dvd_of_mod_eq_zero h
  :294   subst hk
  :295   rw [pow_split_even, pow_split_odd]
  :296   have e : p ^ (n + 1) * k * (p ^ (n + 1) * p ^ n)
  :297       = p ^ (n + 1) * p ^ (n + 1) * (k * p ^ n) := by
  :298     rw [Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_assoc]
  :299     congr 1
  :300     rw [← Nat.mul_assoc, ← Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_comm k (p ^ (n + 1))]
  :301   rw [e]
  :302   exact Nat.mul_mod_right _ _
  :303 
  :304 /-- (4) THE QUOTIENT LANDS IN THE BALL, ALL LEVELS -/
  :305 theorem quotient_general (p n r : Nat) (hp : 0 < p) (h : r % p ^ (n + 1) = 0) :
  :306     (r / p) % p ^ n = 0 := by
  :307   obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ := Nat.dvd_of_mod_eq_zero h
  :308   subst hk
  :309   have e : p ^ (n + 1) * k = p * (p ^ n * k) := by
  :310     rw [Nat.pow_succ, Nat.mul_comm (p ^ n) p, Nat.mul_assoc]
  :311   rw [e, Nat.mul_div_cancel_left _ hp]
  :312   exact Nat.mul_mod_right _ _
  :313 
  :314 theorem pow_split_lead (p n : Nat) : p ^ (2 * n + 1) = p * p ^ (2 * n) := by
  :315   rw [Nat.pow_succ, Nat.mul_comm]
  :316 
  :317 theorem two_n_split (p n : Nat) : p ^ (2 * n) = p ^ n * p ^ n := by
  :318   rw [← Nat.pow_add, Nat.two_mul]
  :319 
  :320 /-- (1) THE SUPPORT SIDE, ALL LEVELS, in mod form (no subtraction anywhere). -/
  :321 theorem ball_pullback_general (p n m j : Nat) (hp : 0 < p) :
  :322     (((p * m + p ^ (2 * n + 1) * j) % p ^ (n + 1) = 0) ↔ (m % p ^ n = 0)) := by
  :323   have hfac : p * m + p ^ (2 * n + 1) * j = p * (m + p ^ n * (p ^ n * j)) := by
  :324     rw [pow_split_lead, two_n_split, Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_add]
  :325   have hmod : (p * m + p ^ (2 * n + 1) * j) % p ^ (n + 1)
  :326       = p * ((m + p ^ n * (p ^ n * j)) % p ^ n) := by
  :327     rw [hfac, Nat.pow_succ, Nat.mul_comm (p ^ n) p, Nat.mul_mod_mul_left]
  :328   rw [hmod, Nat.add_mul_mod_self_left]
  :329   constructor
  :330   · intro h
  :331     rcases Nat.mul_eq_zero.mp h with h0 | h0
  :332     · subst h0; exact absurd hp (Nat.lt_irrefl 0)
  :333     · exact h0
  :334   · intro h
  :335     rw [h, Nat.mul_zero]
  :336 
  :337 theorem rearrange (a p k m : Nat) : a * p * k * p * m = p * p * (a * (k * m)) := by
  :338   calc a * p * k * p * m
  :339       = a * (p * (k * (p * m))) := by
  :340         rw [Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_assoc]
  :341     _ = a * (p * (p * (k * m))) := by rw [Nat.mul_left_comm k p m]
  :342     _ = p * (a * (p * (k * m))) := by rw [Nat.mul_left_comm]
  :343     _ = p * (p * (a * (k * m))) := by rw [Nat.mul_left_comm a p (k * m)]
  :344     _ = p * p * (a * (k * m)) := by rw [← Nat.mul_assoc]
  :345 
  :346 /-- (3) THE TRANSFORM INDEX SHIFT, ALL LEVELS (factor p², no subtraction). -/
  :347 theorem transform_shift_general (p n r m : Nat) (hp : 0 < p)
  :348     (h : r % p ^ (n + 1) = 0) :
  :349     (r * p * m) % p ^ (2 * n + 2) = p ^ 2 * (((r / p) * m) % p ^ (2 * n)) := by
  :350   obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ := Nat.dvd_of_mod_eq_zero h
  :351   subst hk
  :352   have hdiv : p ^ (n + 1) * k / p = p ^ n * k := by
  :353     rw [Nat.pow_succ, Nat.mul_comm (p ^ n) p, Nat.mul_assoc,
  :354       Nat.mul_div_cancel_left _ hp]
  :355   have hp2 : p ^ 2 = p * p := by rw [Nat.pow_succ, Nat.pow_one]
  :356   have hr : p ^ (2 * n + 2) = p * (p * p ^ (2 * n)) := by
  :357     rw [← Nat.mul_assoc, ← hp2, ← Nat.pow_add, Nat.add_comm]
  :358   have hl : p ^ (n + 1) * k * p * m = p * (p * (p ^ n * (k * m))) := by
  :359     rw [Nat.pow_succ, rearrange (p ^ n) p k m, Nat.mul_assoc]
  :360   rw [hdiv, hl, hr, Nat.mul_mod_mul_left, Nat.mul_mod_mul_left, hp2,
  :361     Nat.mul_assoc, Nat.mul_assoc (p ^ n) k m]
  :362

  :57 import Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.TensorProduct
  :58 
  :59 namespace RestrictedTensorLayer1
  :60 
  :61 open scoped TensorProduct
  :62 
  :63 /-- the shape the corpus's `GlobalSectionData` demands of a single local factor:
  :64     a Hilbert space with a unitary `F` and a `parity`, `F² = parity`, `parity² = 1`.
  :65     ### STATED HERE SO LAYER ONE HAS FACTORS TO CONSUME; it is the same shape
  :66     `GlobalSection.GlobalSectionData` carries, and that structure is untouched. -/
  :67 structure FactorData where
  :68   H : Type
  :69   [ncg : NormedAddCommGroup H]
  :70   [ips : InnerProductSpace ℂ H]
  :71   F : H ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] H
  :72   parity : H ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] H
  :73   F_sq : ∀ x, F (F x) = parity x
  :74   parity_sq : ∀ x, parity (parity x) = x
  :75 
  :76 attribute [instance] FactorData.ncg FactorData.ips
  :77 
  :78 variable (A B : FactorData)
  :79 
  :80 /-- the carrier of layer one: the algebraic tensor of the two factors, carrying
  :81     Mathlib's inner product `⟪a ⊗ₜ b, c ⊗ₜ d⟫ = ⟪a, c⟫ * ⟪b, d⟫`. -/
  :82 abbrev Carrier : Type := A.H ⊗[ℂ] B.H
  :83 
  :84 /-- `F` on the tensor: BUILT as `F_A ⊗ F_B` via Mathlib's `congrIsometry`. -/
  :85 noncomputable def Ftensor : Carrier A B ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] Carrier A B :=
  :86   TensorProduct.congrIsometry A.F B.F
  :87 
  :88 /-- `parity` on the tensor: BUILT as `parity_A ⊗ parity_B`. -/
  :89 noncomputable def parityTensor : Carrier A B ≃ₗᵢ[ℂ] Carrier A B :=
  :90   TensorProduct.congrIsometry A.parity B.parity
  :91 
  :92 /-- ### THE GATE, HALF ONE: `F² = parity` on the built object.
  :93     PROVED from the factors', by functoriality of `congr` on pure tensors and
  :94     linearity on the span. -/
  :95 theorem Ftensor_sq (x : Carrier A B) :
  :96     Ftensor A B (Ftensor A B x) = parityTensor A B x := by
  :97   induction x using TensorProduct.induction_on with
  :98   | zero => simp [Ftensor, parityTensor]
  :99   | tmul a b => simp [Ftensor, parityTensor, TensorProduct.congrIsometry, A.F_sq, B.F_sq]
  :100   | add x y hx hy => simp [map_add, hx, hy]
  :101 
  :102 /-- ### THE GATE, HALF TWO: `parity² = 1` on the built object. -/
  :103 theorem parityTensor_sq (x : Carrier A B) :
  :104     parityTensor A B (parityTensor A B x) = x := by
  :105   induction x using TensorProduct.induction_on with
  :106   | zero => simp [parityTensor]
  :107   | tmul a b => simp [parityTensor, TensorProduct.congrIsometry, A.parity_sq, B.parity_sq]
  :108   | add x y hx hy => simp [map_add, hx, hy]
  :109 
  :110 /-- ### LAYER ONE, ASSEMBLED: the two-factor tensor carries the global-section shape.
  :111     ### THE OBJECT IS BUILT, NOT ASSUMED — `F` and `parity` are constructed from the
  :112     ### factors' and the two identities are theorems above, not fields taken on trust. -/
  :113 noncomputable def tensorFactor : FactorData where
  :114   H := Carrier A B
  :115   F := Ftensor A B
  :116   parity := parityTensor A B
  :117   F_sq := Ftensor_sq A B
  :118   parity_sq := parityTensor_sq A B
  :119 
  :120 end RestrictedTensorLayer1

  :123 #print axioms RestrictedTensorLayer1.parityTensor_sq
  :124 #print axioms RestrictedTensorLayer1.tensorFactor
